# MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R001` | **Outbox head-only attempt accounting and rewind.** The outbox increments retry attempts only for the row currently at the head of a stream, so a failing head cannot exhaust the attempt budget of healthy successor rows. A consumer can declare an explicit reject, which dead-letters the head immediately and reports the transition. An administrator can list head status and dead letters. The administrator can also issue a rewind to replay from an earlier position. Caller chain: the outbox consumer's lease call runs `claim` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs` (head test at `head_only_attempt`, line 698). `MutationKernel::outbox_reject` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/operator.rs` calls `reject` at `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/cursor.rs:336`. The operator reaches head status, dead letters and rewind through `Method::MutationOutbox` → `handle_mutation_outbox` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/mutation_outbox.rs:35`) → `operate_outbox` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/views.rs:111`) and `rewind` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rewind.rs:77`). Shape: no new type. The rule lives in `head_only_attempt`, `try_dead_letter_head` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs:535`), `dead_letters` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/dead_letters.rs:26`) and `rewind`, all over the generic `OwnerDomain`, so every outbox owner gets it. Wire views are `OutboxDeadLetterPage` and `OutboxRewindReceipt` of `medulla-mutation-types`. | Outbox tests prove three behaviours. A failing head never dead-letters a healthy successor, and repeated head releases dead-letter only the head. A consumer reject dead-letters immediately, and rewind replays. Tests: `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::a_failing_head_never_dead_letters_a_healthy_successor` (L1); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::reject_dead_letters_the_head_at_once_and_the_stream_continues` (L1); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::rewind_redelivers_in_order_from_a_mid_stream_target` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R002` | **Outbox consumers get per-class topics and reject-driven rebuild.** Outbox consumers publish to semantically distinct topics per message class. A reasoning-projection consumer that rejects a message triggers a rebuild rather than silently acknowledging a skipped projection. `MutationOutbox` exposes an administrative method for inspecting and managing consumer state. The outbox reports metrics on attempts, rejects, dead letters, and rebuilds. Caller chain: the reasoning-projection worker `sweep` in `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/reasoning_projection/recovery.rs` calls `reject_invalid` (line 45) and `rebuild_after_skipped` (line 25) when `settle`/`resolve` of `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs` sees a rejected claim. The operator view is `MutationKernel::outbox_dead_letters` and `outbox_rewind` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/operator.rs`. Metrics register in `crates/medulla-kernel/src/metrics/outbox.rs`. Shape: topics are the `topic` strings validated by `validate_topic` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rows.rs:245`); one consumer name binds one topic (`subscribed_topic`, `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/stream.rs:65`). Counters are `medulla_outbox_dead_lettered_total`, `medulla_outbox_head_age_ms` and `medulla_outbox_head_attempt` in `metrics/outbox.rs`; the rebuild count reuses that file and adds no second registry. | Verified by outbox integration tests asserting per-class topic routing, reject-triggered rebuild of a reasoning projection, and exported outbox metrics. Tests: `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/reasoning_projection.rs::a_dead_lettered_claim_rebuilds_the_projection_before_further_reads` (L2); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::two_topics_retry_exhaustion_preserves_other_topic_attempts_and_progress` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R003` | **Owner-store layout lineage registry with typed upgrade refusal.** The owner-store layout maintains a lineage registry that recognizes every supported on-disk format and refuses an unsupported layout with a named `{LAYOUT}_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` error rather than reinterpreting it silently. A generated document records the steps required to remove a retired layout from the registry. Caller chain: every owner open (`bind_serving_scope`, `crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs:306`) checks the manifest digest against `layout_predecessors` and `pinned_layout_digest` in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs` (lines 266 and 326). The refusal string comes from `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/persisted_layout.rs:45`. The operator command `store-upgrade` (`src/operator_command.rs`) runs the listed upgrades. Shape: `OwnerLayout` and `LayoutPredecessor` in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs` hold the registry (`ALL_LAYOUTS`, 18 layouts). `persisted_layout.rs` derives `{LAYOUT}_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` from the canonical layout name. The removal-steps document is rendered in `lineage.rs` (line 455) through `render_removal_step` of `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/offline_upgrade.rs`; no second table exists. | Verified by a golden lineage test that loads each supported manifest format and checks the typed refusal for unsupported ones. Tests: `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/tests.rs::the_graph_store_open_names_the_command_for_an_upgradable_shard` (L3); `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/tests.rs::every_registered_upgrade_is_one_the_command_can_run` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R004` | **Outbox attempt accounting is scoped to the head row.** Re-leasing an outbox row increments the attempt count only for the row at the head of its stream. Healthy successor rows staged behind it never gain an attempt. A failing head cannot dead-letter its successors. This accounting is implemented in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs`. Caller chain: `claim` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs` re-leases a row and calls `head_only_attempt` (line 698); the dead-letter step is `try_dead_letter_head` (line 535) → `dead_letter` (line 648). The served entry is the reasoning-projection consumer `sweep` in `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs:97`. Shape: the one helper `head_only_attempt(attempt, is_head)` returns the attempt unchanged for a successor and `saturating_add(1)` for the head. The head budget is `MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS` (16) at `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rows.rs:66`. No second counter exists for a successor. | One test asserts a failing head never dead-letters a healthy successor. Another test asserts sixteen re-leases of the head dead-letter only the head. Tests: `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::a_failing_head_never_dead_letters_a_healthy_successor` (L1); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::releasing_the_head_sixteen_times_dead_letters_it` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R005` | **Outbox delivery state remains local to the owning node.** The local store owner writes outbox delivery-side rows. The multi-group raft layer does not replicate them. Delivery progress stays node-local and outside the replicated log. Caller chain: the local owner writes delivery rows through `claim` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs:57`) and `ack` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/cursor.rs:42`) on its own `OwnedStoreHandle`. The raft apply path `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/store/apply.rs` (lines 90 and 117) names outbox authority as owner-local and has no command that carries a delivery row. Shape: no new type. Delivery rows stay in the owner file tables of `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rows.rs`; no `NativeMutationCommand` variant in `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/` carries one. The test reads the follower state through the existing cluster harness of `crates/medulla-server-cluster-tests`. | Confirmed by an integration test checking that outbox delivery rows are not present in replicated state on non-owning cluster members. Test: `crates/medulla-server-cluster-tests/src/split_tests/raft_cluster.rs::outbox_delivery_rows_are_absent_on_non_owning_members` (L3, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006` | **Engine startup does not block on one store's lazy open.** Engine startup must not refuse entirely when the agent-library store fails to open. That store opens lazily. A failure to open it affects only the operations that depend on it, not overall startup. Caller chain: the server boot reaches the Agent Library only through `ensure_agent_library` (`crates/medulla-persistence-records/src/agent_library.rs:709`) → `open_shared_agent_library` (line 724), called from the admin handlers (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/admin/agent.rs:51`) and the projection worker. Boot (`crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs`) never calls it before the listeners bind. Shape: no new type. The lazy open is the `agent_library_open` slot of `ServerState`; a failed open returns `Err(String)` to the one request and leaves the slot empty so the next request retries. The test reuses the boot wiring of `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/medulla_h_admin_boot_regression.rs`. | A startup test forces the agent-library store to fail its open. The test asserts that the engine still starts and serves unrelated operations. Test: `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/medulla_h_admin_boot_regression.rs::boot_serves_unrelated_operations_when_the_agent_library_open_fails` (L3, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.1` | **A clean reopen writes nothing, and a fresh shard is ready in one commit.** Open proves an already-bound serving scope in a read transaction. It begins no write transaction for that proof. The bind refuses a read-only open before that proof, because a bound scope is write authority. Ledger bootstrap checks the declared ledger tables under a write capability, then aborts. It commits nothing and issues no fsync. A fresh owner file binds its control scope inside the one create commit. Create, bind and ledger bootstrap therefore cost one commit. The engine opens the three sidecar stores beside the graph shards. They are `admin-mutations.redb`, `node_info.redb` and `cluster_hierarchy.redb`. A refused open removes every sidecar file that attempt created. Caller chain: `RedbBackend` open (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/backend_open.rs`: `prepare_shards`, line 51, and `open_sidecars`, line 104) → `bind_serving_scope` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs:306`) → `bind_serving_scope_in` (line 605). Shape: the bind proof is a read transaction in `crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs`; the create path reuses the one create commit of the physical layer in `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/`. `open_sidecars` opens `admin-mutations.redb`, `node_info.redb` and `cluster_hierarchy.redb`; a refused open removes the files that attempt created. | Storage-kernel tests reopen a bound store and assert the file bytes stay unchanged after the bind. A create-bound store reports its scope bound without a second commit. A K=8 backend test asserts every sidecar file exists after open. A second test refuses a shard open and asserts no new sidecar file remains. Tests: `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::reopening_a_bound_store_writes_nothing` (L1); `tests/shard_open_budget.rs::sidecars_open_with_the_shards_and_a_refusal_leaves_none` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.2` | **Startup open has a time budget that holds under host load, and each open is diagnosable.** The budget runs in one process on a real disk, beside 16 fsync-writing threads and 32 CPU-burn threads. Under that load a fresh K=8 store opens within 4 s. A small reopened K=8 store opens within 2 s. A populated reopen of about 300 MB on its markers opens within 2.5 s. A separate stress check runs ten copies in parallel, each with its own load. Its looser limits are 60 s fresh and 45 s for each reopen. Every kernel-owned store open logs one line with its duration and phase split. The phases are file open, table validation, recovery scan or skip, bind and ledger. The same split feeds the `medulla_store_open_phase_seconds` histogram. The `medulla_store_open_recovery_scan_total` counter records the scan outcome. Caller chain: `RedbBackend` open (`backend_open.rs`) → store opens in `crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs`; the phase log and histogram come from `crates/medulla-kernel/src/metrics.rs` (`medulla_store_open_phase_seconds`, line 343; `medulla_store_open_recovery_scan_total`, line 350). Shape: the gate is `tests/shard_open_budget.rs` (helpers `check`, `best`, `finish`, scenarios `small_scenario` and `populated_scenario`); no production type is added beyond the phase timers in the kernel open path. | `tests/shard_open_budget.rs` builds the fresh, small and populated K=8 stores under that load. It asserts each budget from the open call's own wall clock, on the fastest of three independent opens: other work on a shared host only adds time to one open, while a real slowdown adds it to all three. It also removes the markers and reopens, to show the full-scan fallback still opens the store. A missed limit prints the captured per-store phase lines. The build host runs the gate ten times in a row with no failure. The ignored stress test runs the ten parallel copies. Tests: `tests/shard_open_budget.rs::shard_open_stays_within_budget_under_load` (L3); `tests/shard_open_budget.rs::populated_reopen_hits_the_marker_fast_path` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.3` | **A clean-shutdown marker lets open skip the full recovery scan.** A graceful close writes a marker file beside the owner file. The marker binds the store incarnation and the physical identity. It also binds the file length, the modification time and the redb header page. That header page carries the last commit. Open skips the row scan of `validate_recovery_content` only when the marker matches the file. A skipped open still checks the persisted root, every scope binding and version row, and every fence's stamped identity. The marker vouches only for content that passed the scan. A session may create the file or commit a write. Its close then re-runs the scan read-only. The close writes the marker only when that scan passes. redb rewrites the header when it opens a file for write. A writable open that found a valid marker therefore stamps it again against that header. The first commit after an open durably removes the marker before redb's commit starts. No commit can become durable while a marker exists. A crash after any commit therefore leaves no valid marker. An aborted write commits nothing, so it leaves the marker in place. The ledger bootstrap on every open is such a write. An absent, torn, copied, restored, upgraded or externally rewritten file runs the full scan. The marker speeds up startup and proves no integrity. Rows tampered with behind a valid marker are out of scope. Caller chain: the owner open → `validate_recovery_content` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/validate.rs:79`) asks `is_valid` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/clean_marker.rs:80`); a graceful close writes the marker at the path of `marker_path` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/clean_marker.rs:50`). Shape: `CleanMarker` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/clean_marker.rs:103`) binds incarnation, identity, file length, modification time and the redb header page. The first-commit removal sits in the write authority of `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/write_authority.rs`. | Storage-kernel tests cover each case. A clean reopen reports the scan skipped. A missing marker, a torn marker, a copied marker and an external commit each run the scan. Beginning a write keeps the marker. The commit removes it, and the next close restores it. An aborted write keeps a valid marker. An aborted ledger bootstrap keeps it, and the next open still skips the scan. A child process killed by `abort()` after a write leaves no marker, so the reopen scans. A child killed without writing keeps its still-true marker. A store closed with a foreign fence writes no marker. A forged matching marker still lets the fence check refuse the reopen. Tests: `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::a_store_with_no_marker_runs_the_full_scan` (L1); `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::the_first_commit_after_open_removes_the_marker_before_committing` (L1); `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::a_process_killed_after_a_write_leaves_no_valid_marker` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.4` | **Pre-listener boot does not block on write-lock-holding maintenance.** The projection drain never holds the global write lock across the agent-library store open. The worker ensures the store outside the lock. Publishing the ensured handle takes one short write. A re-check lets a concurrent ensurer win. Per-row critical sections stay short. No write spans a projection graph commit. Each non-empty sweep logs one INFO line: rows claimed, graphs committed, elapsed. The worker never performs minutes of work silently. The matview reload and the ts reconciliation close RAM re-hydration and crash-window residuals. Nothing requires them to precede serving. They run as background tasks spawned after the primary listeners bind. Each logs its start and completion: counts and elapsed. Code roots: `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs`, `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs`, `crates/medulla-server/src/transport.rs`, `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs`, `src/main.rs`. Caller chain: `serve_transports` in `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs` (lines 808 and 830) calls `spawn_post_bind_maintenance` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs:35`) after the listeners bind; the projection worker `run` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs:77`) ensures the store in `open_agent_library` (line 153) outside the write lock. Shape: no new type. `sweep` (line 97) logs one INFO line per non-empty sweep; `spawn_matview_reload` and `spawn_time_series_reconcile` in `boot_maintenance.rs` are the background tasks. | The test seeds N pending import rows on a temp durable store. It boots the real wiring: projection worker, UDS listener, post-bind maintenance. It asserts the listener binds within 30s and before the drain completes. It asserts the drain finishes within a bounded wait. Test: `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/medulla_h_admin_boot_regression.rs::boot_listener_binds_before_the_projection_drain_completes` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.5` | **Ts startup reconciliation replays the legacy gap once, then uses the cursor fast path.** A legacy store lacks durable projection cursors. Every series then takes the slow path once. The slow path is a full range scan plus exact multiset point diff. The pass logs its start: series count per authoritative shard. It logs completion: series examined and reconciled, points replayed, cursors written, elapsed. It logs even when the work is zero. `mark_projection_ready` persists the (count, min_ts, max_ts) cursor durably. Subsequent boots skip a converged series without a point scan. The replay never duplicates a point. The move off the pre-listener path does not change diff correctness. Code roots: `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/timeseries.rs`, `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs`. Caller chain: `spawn_post_bind_maintenance` → `spawn_time_series_reconcile` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs`) → `reconcile_time_series` (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/timeseries.rs:186`) → `reconcile_shard` (line 140) → `reconcile_series` (line 89). Shape: the cursor is the `(count, min_ts, max_ts)` triple written by `mark_projection_ready` in `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/timeseries.rs`; the diff is the existing exact multiset diff of `reconcile_series`. | It seeds a series durable in the authoritative shard with an empty served store. It asserts the reconciliation replays exactly the seeded points within a bounded wait. It asserts a second reconciliation is a true no-op: zero replay, no duplicates. Test: `crates/medulla-persistence-tests/src/redb_backend_tests.rs::ts_reconcile_replays_seeded_points_once_then_is_a_no_op` (L2, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R007` | **Host binary exposes offline store, archive and restore commands.** The host binary `medulla-server` provides `store-upgrade inspect` and `store-upgrade apply` in `src/operator_command.rs`. They examine and migrate on-disk layout state outside normal server startup. The binary also provides the `archive` tool and the `restore` options `--to-seq` and `--to-time` (`MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R007.1`). Caller chain: `medulla-server store-upgrade inspect|apply` → `Cli::run` in `src/operator_command.rs:65` → the store-upgrade report of `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/`; `restore` and `migrate-shards` dispatch through `run_if_requested` (`src/offline_tools.rs`). Shape: the `TOOLS` table in `src/offline_tools.rs` lists the offline tools; `src/operator_command.rs` owns `store-upgrade`. The `archive` tool and the restore targets are specified by R007.1; nothing else is added here. | Verified by a CLI integration test exercising `store-upgrade inspect` and `store-upgrade apply` against a sample data directory. Tests: `tests/operator_command_store_upgrade_cli.rs::storage_inspect_reports_nothing_to_do_on_an_empty_data_directory` (L3); `tests/operator_command_store_upgrade_cli.rs::storage_apply_without_confirm_is_refused_and_opens_nothing` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R007.1` | **Offline tools archive and restore to a target.** Stage: durable kernel, offline tools. Caller chain (restore): `medulla-server restore --bundle B --persist-dir D --shards K --archive <backend-spec> --to-seq N | --to-time RFC3339` → `src/offline_tools/restore.rs::run` → `backup::restore_bundle` → `recovery::archive::replay_to`. Caller chain (archive): `medulla-server archive --persist-dir D --to <backend-spec>` → new `src/offline_tools/archive.rs::run`, registered in `TOOLS` of `src/offline_tools.rs`. Restore keeps its rule that the destination holds no shard files. Without a target it behaves as today. Consumer: `MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R016.2`. Caller chain: `medulla-server restore` → `run` (`src/offline_tools/restore.rs:47`) → `restore_bundle` (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/backup.rs:801`) → `replay_to` in the new `crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/archive.rs`; `medulla-server archive` → the new `src/offline_tools/archive.rs::run`. Shape: the archive tool is a new file beside `restore.rs` and a new entry in `TOOLS` of `src/offline_tools.rs`. `replay_to(archive, seq_or_time)` lives with the recovery modules of `crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/` and reuses the bundle manifest read of `backup.rs`. `restore_bundle` keeps its fresh-directory rule and gains an optional target argument. | Test: `tests/offline_restore_pitr_cli.rs::target_past_archive_refuses_and_writes_nothing` (L3, planned). Test: `tests/offline_restore_pitr_cli.rs::target_past_archive_refuses_and_writes_nothing` (L3, planned). |

A CI job compiles and runs the raft suite on every change. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R009` | **Native raft commands are sealed as their bare method.** Native consensus commands are sealed and opened as the bare method they represent. Shape: `SealedNativeMethod` in `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/sealed.rs` is the one sealing type; the bare method is the sealed payload, so no envelope field can mismatch. | Verified by a follower catch-up test that replays a create-graph-then-write sequence and by unit tests on `SealedNativeMethod` sealing and opening. Tests: `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/tests.rs::native_envelopes_reject_empty_unsealed_malformed_oversize_and_wrong_key` (L1); `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/tests.rs::work_item_mutations_round_trip_through_sealed_native_command` (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R018` | **UDF registry and response-dedup cache are tenant-scoped.** The UDF registry is keyed and scoped by tenant and actor through a single authorization chokepoint. A tenant cannot invoke or shadow another tenant's UDF. This follows the foreign-source scoping in MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R013. The in-memory mutation response-dedup cache keys its entries by tenant and caller. It never replays one tenant's cached response to another. Caller chain: served `RegisterUdf` and `RunUdf` → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-compute/src/wasm_udf.rs:17`) → `register_udf` (line 26) / `run_udf` (line 57) under the `OwnerScopedCall` owner. The owner scope of the UDF registry is the `(tenant_scope, actor_scope)` pair of `crates/medulla-server-api/src/udf_catalog.rs`; the dedup key is built in `response_dedup_key` from tenant, caller and request id. | Cross-tenant negative tests assert that UDF invoke and shadow attempts are denied. They also assert that the response-dedup cache never replays another tenant's cached response. Tests: `crates/medulla-server-api/src/udf_catalog.rs::udf_ids_are_tenant_scoped_for_run_and_shadowing` (L1); `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation/context.rs::dedup_key_separates_tenants_and_actors_but_keeps_retries` (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R020` | **Bounded background scrub detects node corruption between restarts.** A background content scrub reads nodes using a read transaction with no write lock, resumes from a stored key cursor, runs at a bounded rate, and surfaces typed corruption findings on status and metrics, rather than relying on detection only at read time or the next restart. Unseal failures return a typed error instead of an untyped string. The typed unseal error is `NodeUnreadable`. | Verified by a test that corrupts a persisted node, runs the scrub, and asserts a typed finding is reported and the cursor resumes correctly after restart. Test: `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/scrub_api_tests.rs::scrub_cursor_survives_reopen_and_the_walk_skips_nothing` (L2). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R022` | **Just-in-time RBAC elevation requires two-person approval.** A dedicated `rbac.elevation` control lease is consulted by the access-check path to grant temporary elevated access, requires approval from two distinct principals, carries a hard expiry, is audited, and is never issued automatically by a learning or inference process. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R023` | **UQL enforces a read-only guarantee at compile time and by test.** `UnifiedQuery`, `UnifiedQueryText`, and `Uql` operations never perform a mutation or touch a durable domain, are idempotent, and execute against a `Snapshot`; a compile-time pin ensures operation dispatch takes the snapshot by shared reference so a mutable path cannot be introduced without a build failure. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R031.1` | **The audit append accounts the serving write version.** After an audit append, the serving write version equals the authoritative scope version. Each attempt commits one maintenance batch under a unique attempt id. The kernel advances the authoritative version by one per committed batch, maintenance included. The served append accounts that commit in the serving projection. A governed ingest stamps its graph version expectation from the serving version. That write commits after the append. It never meets the +1 skew the kernel refuses as `STALE_VERSION`. Caller chain: served `Method::AuditAppend` → `crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/native_routes.rs:683` → `dispatch_helpers.rs` (line 697 accounts the serving version after every successful append, replay included). It performs one audit append, then the write stamped from the serving version. It asserts the serving and authoritative versions agree. It asserts the stamped write commits through the kernel version check. It asserts a replayed append advances the serving version by exactly one. After that, the two agree again. Test: `crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/dispatch_helpers.rs::an_audit_append_accounts_the_serving_version_for_stamped_writes` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R031.2` | **Writes are stamped from the authoritative scope version.** A durable commit can advance a graph's mutation scope without the serving projection accounting it. The authoritative version read raises the serving write version to the durable one exactly once. This precedes any new version stamp. SourceIngest stamps its batch from that read. A fresh single-writer ingest after such a commit therefore commits. It never meets the kernel's `STALE_VERSION` refusal. A serving version ahead of its authority is still refused. Caller chain: a governed `SourceIngest` stamp → `authoritative_graph_version` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/`) → the persistence version read, raising the serving `GraphCore` version. Shape: the one function `authoritative_graph_version(persistence, graph, core)` in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/`; SourceIngest calls it before stamping. | Test `the_authoritative_version_accounts_a_durable_write_the_projection_missed` in `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation_batch/tests.rs` puts the projection two versions behind the durable scope. The read returns the durable version and the projection equals it. A projection ahead of its authority refuses. Test: `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation_batch/tests.rs::the_authoritative_version_accounts_a_durable_write_the_projection_missed` (L2). |

Tests: `crates/medulla-types/src/plan_cache_key.rs::schema_version_advance_invalidates_the_cached_plan` (L1); `crates/medulla-types/src/plan_cache_key.rs::a_candidate_for_a_different_statement_is_never_reused` (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070` | **Operator command inspects and applies registered offline store upgrades.** The server binary has one operator command with two verbs over a data directory. `inspect` reads the format of every store and writes nothing. Its exit status says one of four things. Nothing needs doing, or an upgrade is available. A store may be in a format this build neither opens nor upgrades. A store may also be unreadable. `apply` requires an explicit confirmation flag and refuses while an engine holds the directory. It runs each registered offline upgrade in place and keeps the existing rows. It stops at the first failure and leaves that store in its previous layout. It never writes a store it does not upgrade. A second run reports nothing to do. The upgrades come from one registry in the storage kernel, so a new upgrade is one entry. With automatic migration off (`MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070.1`), the engine refuses to start on a store that a registered upgrade applies to. The refusal carries the named format error of that store and the exact command to run. No other store state refuses startup this way. Any other failure of a lazily opened store stays local to that store. | Command tests run on disposable stores. Inspect on current stores reports nothing to do and writes nothing. Inspect on a registered predecessor reports an available upgrade. Apply upgrades graph-shard, Agent Library and SQL catalog predecessors and keeps the exact row bytes. A second apply is a no-op. Both verbs refuse while the engine lock of the directory is held. Apply without confirmation opens nothing. An undeclared or non-upgradable format is reported and stays byte-identical. A failed upgrade stops the run and leaves later stores untouched. Apply still upgrades a predecessor that an unclean shutdown left. The startup check and the ordinary store opens refuse a predecessor with the named error and the command. Storage-kernel tests prove that every registry entry is a declared predecessor. They also prove that each entry reaches the current layout exactly once. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070.1` | **The engine migrates an older supported store when it opens it.** `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate.rs` runs the registered upgrades of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070` through `apply_store`. `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs:155` calls it right after the engine takes its exclusive directory lock, before any store opens. The function takes the held lock as a parameter. It writes a journal under `<data-dir>/.store-upgrade-staging` before the first upgrade and replaces it atomically after each one. Each upgrade is one atomic commit, so an interrupted run leaves every store in one layout. The next open finds the journal, classifies every store again and resumes. A second run on current stores writes nothing. Each step is logged and counted in `medulla_store_migration_steps_total{step,outcome}`. A declared predecessor with no upgrade is refused as `STORE_FORMAT_TOO_OLD`. A layout outside the lineage is refused as `STORE_FORMAT_TOO_NEW`. The engine writes neither of them. A graph shard in either state stops startup; another store keeps its own lazy-open refusal. `MEDULLA_STORE_AUTO_MIGRATE` is on by default. `false` turns migration off, and an unknown value is refused as `STORE_AUTO_MIGRATE_INVALID_SWITCH`. Caller chain: server boot `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs:322` (switch) and `:331` (call, after the directory lock) → `migrate_on_open` (`crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate.rs:145`) → `apply_store`. Shape: `migrate_on_open` takes the held `PersistDirLock`; the journal lives under `<data-dir>/.store-upgrade-staging`; the metric is `medulla_store_migration_steps_total{step,outcome}`. The switch parser is `auto_migrate_enabled` of the store-upgrade module. No second runner exists. | `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate_tests.rs` tests disposable predecessor stores. Migration under the held lock keeps the row bytes and clears the journal. A second run writes nothing. A run stopped after one store leaves the journal, and the next open resumes it. With the switch off, the old named refusal and command stay. A too-new shard and a too-old blob store are refused by code and stay byte-identical. The metric samples render. Tests: `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate_tests.rs::open_migrates_a_previous_patch_store_under_the_held_lock_and_keeps_rows` (L2); `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate_tests.rs::an_interrupted_migration_resumes_at_the_next_open` (L2). |

The gateway overwrites `tx_now` with engine time before the method is logged, so replay uses the logged value. An edge gets `tx_from = tx_now`. Invalidation sets `valid_until` and `tx_to`. A caller provides `valid_from`; it defaults to `tx_now`. No separate stamping method exists. An interval with `valid_until` before `valid_from` refuses with `VALIDITY_INVERTED`, registered in `ServerErrorCode`. Every stamp passes this single write chokepoint. While a kernel runs, the executor polls job status every `min(5000 ms, lease_ms / 3)` and renews the lease otherwise. A cancel request stops the kernel and the job ends `Cancelled` with reason `kernel_cancelled`. A passed deadline ends `Failed` with `deadline_exceeded`. An exhausted CPU budget ends `Failed` with `cpu_budget_exceeded`. No lease renewal happens after the stop. Shape: today the tick is a fixed 250 ms interval (`executor_claim.rs:87`) and the renewal passes a fixed 60 000 ms (`executor_claim.rs:150`). This row derives both from the claim's lease as `min(5000, lease_ms / 3)`. The stop reasons `deadline_exceeded` and `cpu_budget_exceeded` already exist at `executor_claim.rs:140`. The cancelled branch calls `mark_cancelled_fenced` with no reason, and the row adds `kernel_cancelled`. Reuse `JobStore::fail_attempt_fenced` (`crates/medulla-jobs/src/store.rs:1084`). | Planned: `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs/graph_tests.rs::cancel_deadline_and_cpu_budget_each_stop_the_kernel_with_their_reason` (L3) uses a long synthetic kernel for all three. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R084.2` | **Behavior: write-conflict resolver.** Stage: durable kernel, served `ApplyChangeEnvelope`. Code: add `pub fn resolve_write_conflict(conflict: &WriteConflict, strategy: MergeStrategy, current: &BTreeMap<String, Value>, incoming: &BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, Value>, WriteConflict>` to `crates/medulla-types/src/write_conflict.rs`. The name differs from the query-planner symbol `resolve_conflict`. Rule: `Refuse` returns the conflict. `PreferNewer` returns `incoming`. `FieldUnion` keeps every key that only one side holds and every key with equal values on both sides. A key with different values on both sides returns the conflict. The function is pure and deterministic. Refusals: it returns the input `WriteConflict`; R084.4 maps it to a code. Shape: `WriteConflict` and `MergeStrategy` are in `crates/medulla-types/src/write_conflict.rs`. The new function is pure and sits beside them. The value type is the `BTreeMap<String, Value>` that `core.get_node_properties` decodes. Writes: none. Depends on: R084.1. | Planned: `crates/medulla-types/src/write_conflict.rs::tests::resolve_under_each_strategy` (L1) covers each strategy, one disjoint union and one colliding key. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R084.3` | **Wiring: version check calls the resolver on the served envelope path.** Stage: durable kernel, served `ApplyChangeEnvelope` and `ApplyChangeEnvelopes`. Add `resolve_envelope_conflict(envelope, conflict, strategy, core) -> Result<ChangeEnvelope, WriteConflict>`. The strategy comes from the new field `write_conflict_strategy` on the server state, set from `Args` in `src/main.rs`. Under `Refuse` the function returns the conflict at once. Under another strategy, every operation must be `Method::AddNode` on an existing node, else the conflict stands. For each such operation the function decodes the current properties with `core.get_node_properties`, calls `resolve_write_conflict` and replaces `properties_msgpack`. It then sets `VersionExpectation::Graph(actual)` and calls `reseal_envelope`. A known replay skips the check, as today. The kernel check `STALE_VERSION` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/commit.rs::admit_fresh` stays the last guard. Shape: `reseal_envelope` is `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/mutation_batch/model/batch.rs:215`. `GraphCore::get_node_properties` is `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_readthrough.rs:11`. The strategy field lives in the server state built from `ServerArgs`. Writes: the existing envelope commit only. The Rust `ServerErrorCode` is the only source, and the hand-written client follows it (`MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R001.4`). Rule: under `Refuse` the response is `WRITE_CONFLICT: expected {e}, actual {a}, scope {s}`. Under a merge strategy, two cases return `WRITE_CONFLICT_UNMERGEABLE` with the same three values. One is an operation that is not `AddNode` on an existing node. The other is a field collision. Both refusals change no graph state, no version and no outbox row. Evidence: R084.5. The cursor is a signed token over the last `doc_id`, the tenant, graph incarnation, principal, policy digest and filter digest. It uses HMAC-SHA256 under the server auth secret. Config `memory_sync.cursor_ttl_s`, default 900, bounds [60, 86400], in `crates/medulla-types/src/memory_sync.rs::MemorySyncConfig`. A list never reveals hidden counts, identifiers or digests. Refusals: `MEMSYNC_CURSOR_INVALID`, `MEMSYNC_CURSOR_EXPIRED`, `MEMSYNC_NOT_AVAILABLE`. Shape: the read arm extends `try_handle_memory_maintenance` in `memory.rs:18`. The cursor starts from `CursorFamily::encode` and `decode` (`crates/medulla-types/src/tenant_cursor.rs:32` and `:45`). The HMAC signature follows the `Hmac<Sha256>` use in `crates/medulla-server-auth/src/auth.rs`. Writes: none. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.3` | **Behavior: sync decisions and compare-and-set.** Stage: durable kernel, served `SyncMemorySnapshot`. Caller chain: `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs:166`) → `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/memory_snapshot.rs::decide_sync` (new). Pure core: `crates/medulla-types/src/memory_sync.rs::decide(head, request) -> Decision`. `ObserveFile` compares the observed digest with the head and answers `Observed`; it writes nothing. `Submit { expected_revision: Option<u64>, content, content_digest }` accepts when `expected_revision` equals the head revision, or the document is absent and `expected_revision` is `None`. The new revision is the head revision plus 1 and its parent is the old head. A submit with the head digest returns `Accepted` with no new revision. A stale submit keeps its content as a retained candidate, leaves the head unchanged and returns `ConflictRetained`. `Resolve { candidate_revision, expected_head_revision, resolution }` has `KeepHead`, `TakeCandidate` or `Merged { content, content_digest }`. It applies only when `expected_head_revision` equals the head and the candidate is retained. `Acknowledge` is R085.6. A repeated `request_id` returns the stored outcome for `memory_sync.receipt_ttl_s`, default 86400, bounds [3600, 2592000]. Shape: the pure core is `decide` in `crates/medulla-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. The write path reuses `commit_gateway` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest/graph.rs:15`) as `try_handle_memory` does (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs:315`). | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.6` | **Evidence: provenance and materialization acknowledgment.** Stage: durable kernel, served `SyncMemorySnapshot` `Acknowledge` and the `Observed` outcome. Caller chain: as R085.4, then `decide_sync` (R085.3) in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/memory_snapshot.rs` (new) for the `Acknowledge` action. Code: `Acknowledge { doc_id, revision, content_digest, observation_generation: u64 }` in `crates/medulla-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. The handler stores the acknowledgment as a record separate from the accepted content. Rule: state is `InSync` only when three values match. The acknowledged revision, digest and generation must equal the head revision, the head digest and the latest observation generation. No unacknowledged submit or unresolved conflict may exist. A pending edit or conflict gives `Pending` or `Conflict`. Missing evidence gives `Unavailable`. Each revision persists actor, policy digest, request id, parent revision, content digest and outcome. Shape: `Acknowledge` and `SyncState` are new in `crates/medulla-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. The acknowledgment record is a node `memack:<doc_id>` in the same `MutationBatch` as R085.4. Writes: the R085.4 gateway commit. Depends on: R085.3, R085.4. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::provenance_and_materialization_ack_survive_restart` (L3) asserts each state, the stored provenance and the same state after an engine restart. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.7` | **Retention: conflicts and revisions survive restart and collection.** Stage: durable kernel, served `SyncMemorySnapshot`. Caller chain: as R085.4 for writes; the existing blob collector in `crates/medulla-server-blob/src/blob/store/gc.rs` for release. Code: `HolderNamespace` is a validated string newtype (`crates/medulla-server-blob/src/blob/store/holders.rs:80`), so the namespace is `HolderNamespace::new("memory_snapshot")` and no enum variant is added. Each revision and each retained candidate holds its blob as holder `<doc_id>@<revision>`. Rule: an unresolved conflict never expires. A resolved candidate and a superseded revision release after `memory_sync.history_retention_s`, default 2592000, bounds [3600, 31536000]. A document keeps at most `memory_sync.max_revisions_per_doc` revisions, default 64, bounds [1, 4096]. A tenant holds at most `memory_sync.capacity_bytes`, default 1073741824, bounds [1048576, 1099511627776]. A write over capacity returns `MEMSYNC_RETENTION_EXHAUSTED` before any effect. Release needs an authorized policy and a passed deadline. Config lives in `MemorySyncConfig`, set by `Args` in `src/main.rs`. Shape: holders use `HolderChange::acquire` and `release` (`holders.rs:113` and `:125`) and `HolderReconcile` (`holders.rs:162`). The handler crate `medulla-h-graph` gains a `medulla-server-blob` dependency, as `medulla-h-admin` has in `connector_pack/import.rs`. `MemorySyncConfig` is new in `crates/medulla-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. Depends on: R085.4. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::conflicts_survive_gc_and_retention_refusal` (L3) runs a collector pass and a restart, then asserts retained candidates and the exhaustion refusal. |

Journey: read and list, two competing saves, conflict retention, one resolution, acknowledgment, engine restart, independent readback. Negatives: cross-tenant, wrong graph, stale cursor, denied writer, replayed request id. Transport, dispatch, authorization and persistence are real. Benchmark: nightly group `decide/memory_scaling` (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R178.1`) covers growth; a submit of 64 KiB must finish within 10 ms at p99. Shape: the root test file `tests/memory_sync_served.rs` is new and uses `test_support::durable_state` for restart. The nightly group `decide/memory_scaling` is in the decide bench set. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R086.3` | **Behavior: cache-miss read serves durable bytes without growing RAM.** Stage: durable kernel, served `GetNodeProperties`. Caller chain: `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/pipeline.rs:408`) → `handle_get_node_properties` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/nodes.rs:89`, called at `nodes.rs:203`) → `GraphCore::get_node_properties` (`crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_readthrough.rs:11`) → `read_through_get` (`core_readthrough.rs:24`); the checked variant `get_node_properties_checked` (new, R086.1) replaces the `get_node_properties` call. Rule: serve resident bytes first. On a miss, clone the read-through out of its lock, release the lock, then read the durable tier. Do not repopulate RAM. A Bloom negative may skip the read only when `bloom_complete` is set. Under `Unavailable`, the handler answers `DURABLE_READ_UNAVAILABLE` and never an empty result. Shape: the lock-scoped clone and the Bloom guard exist in `read_through_get` (`core_readthrough.rs:24`) and stay. The handler maps `Unavailable` to `DURABLE_READ_UNAVAILABLE` at `nodes.rs:89`. Writes: none. Caller chain: authenticated `dispatch` → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `try_handle_gateway` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway.rs:57`) → `try_handle` (`gateway_graph_routes.rs:166`) → `try_handle_lifecycle` (`gateway_graph_routes.rs:541`, arm at `:554`) → `apply_evict_lru` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph.rs:121`) → `PersistenceBackend::durable_node_presence` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/api/persistence.rs:694`; Redb impl `trait_graph.rs:82`). Rule: a candidate stays resident in four cases. The persistence backend is absent. `durable_node_presence` returns an error. The result length differs from the candidate count. The entry is `false`. Shape: the four keep-resident cases are the no-backend branch, the `Ok(_) | Err(_)` arm, the length guard and the `present.then_some` filter in `apply_evict_lru` (`gateway_graph.rs:121`). `EVICTION_UNCONFIRMED` is a new response detail field. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.1` | **Run checkpoint binds short-term and long-term memory references.** Subsystem: work items. Caller chain: served `Method::PublishRunCheckpoint` → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item.rs:40`) → `commit_verified_work_item` (`work_item.rs:104`) → the MutationBatch apply in `crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:415` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint.rs::publish` (`checkpoint.rs:149`). The request already carries `short_term` and `long_term` in `CheckpointContent`, each a list of `{document_id, revision, digest}`. `publish` reads every referenced document through the R085 snapshot store and refuses a missing document or a digest mismatch. The checkpoint pointer and its R085 snapshot commit in one `MutationBatch`. Its expected-version precondition carries the WorkItem lease epoch and fencing token. Short-term memory keeps at most 64 revisions per run and expires when the WorkItem completes. Long-term memory follows R085 retention. Refusal codes: `CHECKPOINT_STALE_REVISION`, `CHECKPOINT_FENCE_STALE`, `CHECKPOINT_MEMORY_REF_MISSING`, `CHECKPOINT_DIGEST_MISMATCH`. Registered in `ServerErrorCode`. Consumers: ARBORA-A2A-R001.2 and MERISTEM-BOUNDARY-R043.8. Children `.2` and `.3` deliver the rest. Shape: `CheckpointMemoryRef`, `CheckpointContent` and `PublishRunCheckpointRequest` are in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/run_checkpoint.rs:10`. `publish` copies both lists into the stored snapshot (`checkpoint.rs:272`). This row adds the existence and digest checks in `publish` and the four refusal codes in `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`. Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.1, R085.3, R085.4. | Test: `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::checkpoint_native_authenticated_cas_replay_and_owner_isolation` (L3) covers publish CAS and owner binding today. Planned: `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::checkpoint_with_memory_refs_commits_atomically_and_survives_restart` (L3) publishes, restarts and reads identical digests. `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::checkpoint_refuses_stale_fence_and_missing_memory_ref` (L3) asserts each code and an unchanged head. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.1.1` | **Native context-bound checkpoint transaction.** `PublishRunCheckpoint` appends a private sealed snapshot and updates its WorkItem pointer atomically through MutationBatch. Verify graph, tenant, run, WorkItem node type and admitted owner; bind context to the immutable original `RUN_ADMISSION_REQUEST.conversation.context_id` when present. Any original `metadata.a2a_context_id` must agree; legacy admissions use only that original metadata binding. Never use replaceable WorkItem metadata. The live native lease epoch/fence and expected checkpoint revision govern new writes. Live-claim and head-CAS refusals retain `CONFLICT`; exact nonce reuse retains `REPLAY_NONCE_CONSUMED`. Canonical retry identity and body bind context; an identical historical retry with fresh authenticated nonce reconstructs the accepted receipt after lease expiry, while changed content, memory references or fence under the same identity conflict. `GetRunCheckpoint` reads owner/context binding, WorkItem and exact checkpoint together in one native snapshot. Bounded UTF-8 JSON content and separate memory references are validated, not executed. The checkpoint-only graph table has an explicit offline predecessor migration. Caller chain: served `Method::PublishRunCheckpoint` → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item.rs:40`) → `commit_verified_work_item` (`work_item.rs:104`) → the MutationBatch apply in `crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:415` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint.rs::publish` (`checkpoint.rs:149`); read side: served `Method::GetRunCheckpoint` → `WorkItemRead::Checkpoint` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item_read.rs:36`) → `RedbBackend` read at `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/work_item_read.rs:285` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint.rs::read_current` (`checkpoint.rs:302`). Shape: the request and snapshot types and `CheckpointContent::validate` are in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/run_checkpoint.rs`. Owner binding is `run_owner::owned` inside `publish` (`checkpoint.rs:149`). The row reuses both and adds no second checkpoint path. | Tests: `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::checkpoint_native_authenticated_cas_replay_and_owner_isolation` (L3). `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint/tests.rs::checkpoint_cas_is_atomic_private_and_owner_bound` (L2). `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint/tests.rs::aborted_transaction_retains_neither_snapshot_nor_pointer` (L2). `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint/tests.rs::mutable_metadata_cannot_rebind_checkpoint_context` (L2). Native execution receipts stay separate under R028.1.3. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.1.2` | **Checkpoint retention, fenced resume and stream cursor.** Subsystem: work items. Caller chain: served `Method::PublishRunCheckpoint` → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item.rs:40`) → `commit_verified_work_item` (`work_item.rs:104`) → the MutationBatch apply in `crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:415` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint.rs::publish` (`checkpoint.rs:149`); and served `Method::GetRunCheckpoint` → `WorkItemRead::Checkpoint` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item_read.rs:36`) → `RedbBackend` read at `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/work_item_read.rs:285` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint.rs::read_current` (`checkpoint.rs:302`). Children `.1` to `.3` deliver three rules. `.1` retention: pruning keeps the newest 64 short-term revisions per run, inside the commit that adds the 65th. `.2` memory binding: refs name an existing R085 revision of the same tenant and graph. `.3` resume fence: `GetRunCheckpoint` returns the head to the holder of the current lease epoch and token. Others get `CHECKPOINT_FENCE_STALE`. A current-run checkpoint wins over history. Stream cursors `(run_id, stream_id)` belong to the host in ARBORA-A2A-R010; medulla stores none. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R043.7` | **Attested envelope text admits `@` and `\` and refuses only host identity.** `ChangeEnvelope::validate` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope.rs` screens caller-attested material values with the attested rule. These values are graph operation properties, compare-and-set conditions and updates, outbox payloads, evidence loci and feature values. The attested rule is in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs`. The rule SHALL admit ordinary document text that contains `@` or `\`. Examples are decorators, handles, version pins, LaTeX, escapes and Windows program paths. The rule SHALL refuse a host path that starts anywhere in the text. The host-path prefixes are the home, users, mnt and root prefixes in `HOST_PATH_PREFIXES`. The rule SHALL also refuse a local file URI, a Windows profile path (`<drive>:\Users\`) and an e-mail address. Property keys and envelope identifiers keep the strict character rule. Repository-snapshot material keeps its own rule. Caller chain: served `ApplyChangeEnvelope` → `apply_one_change_envelope_inner` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/change_envelope.rs:611`) → the commit in `commit_change_envelope_with_budget` (`crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_mutation/change_envelope.rs:35`) → `ChangeEnvelope::validate` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope.rs:252`) → `validate_attested_text` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs:105`). Shape: the rule is `HOST_PATH_PREFIXES` (`material.rs:56`) with `has_host_path_anywhere` (`material.rs:83`), `has_windows_profile_path` (`material.rs:94`) and `has_email` (`material.rs:77`). Repository-snapshot text keeps `validate_repository_text` (`material.rs:119`). | Tests: `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs::tests::attested_material_admits_ordinary_document_text` (L1) and `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs::tests::attested_material_still_refuses_host_identity` (L1). `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/tests.rs::test_served_change_envelope_admits_document_text_and_codes_refusals` (L3) commits document text with `@` and `\`. It sends `ApplyChangeEnvelopeDraft`, which continues as `ApplyChangeEnvelope`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R043.8` | **Envelope admission refusals carry a declared code and a safe diagnostic.** Every refusal that `ChangeEnvelope::validate` returns SHALL carry a declared error code. Host-identity refusals and the strict identifier rule emit `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. The detail names the shape found (host path, file URI or e-mail address) and the material class. The detail SHALL NOT echo the refused value. `contract::classify_refusal` classifies any other free-text refusal as `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. The served path never reports it as `INTERNAL: unclassified engine refusal`. `Response::err` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/support_04.rs` SHALL log dropped text at debug level. Caller chain: served `ApplyChangeEnvelope` → `apply_one_change_envelope_inner` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/change_envelope.rs:611`; refusal mapping at `change_envelope.rs:731`) → `classify_refusal` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/error_registry.rs:48`) → `Response::err` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/support_04.rs:140`). Shape: `ChangeEnvelope::validate` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope.rs:252`) wraps each refusal with `classify_refusal`. The diagnostic text comes from `host_identity_refusal` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs:100`), which names the shape and class and never the value. | Tests: `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs::tests::host_identity_refusals_are_coded_and_do_not_echo_the_value` (L1). `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/tests.rs::test_served_change_envelope_admits_document_text_and_codes_refusals` (L3) asserts `error == INVALID_ARGUMENT` and a privacy detail that names the e-mail shape. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.2` | **Read the committed terminal event through the existing WorkItem outcome read.** `GetWorkItemOutcome` retains `work:read`, verified tenant and graph authorization. The fenced terminal CAS binds the engine-derived outbox batch and stored event byte digest to the WorkItem. One snapshot resolves that binding to the exact committed run-event row and exposes its scope, commit sequence, ordinal and safe result references. Legacy rows return explicit `legacy_unbound` unavailability; a missing or corrupt bound event refuses. No lease authority is projected. This point read excludes status/step history and lifecycle streaming. | Commit a real terminal extension, read its event, reopen storage and read the identical identity. Foreign tenants see nothing. Corrupt/missing bound events refuse. Legacy rows report unavailability. Generic writes cannot forge the native binding. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.3` | **Fenced metadata CAS requires a live worker lease.** Subsystem: work items. Caller: served `Method::CasWorkItemMetadata` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs::apply_work_item_rows` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/lease.rs`. When the request carries `expected_lease`, the transaction compares the stored lease expiry with the engine mutation timestamp. The call refuses when the timestamp is at or after expiry, or when expiry is absent, with `CONFLICT: lease not live`. Caller time never extends the fence. A request without `expected_lease` keeps the unfenced behavior. Rows stay unchanged on refusal. Shape: the lease clause is the `expected_lease` arm of `cas_work_item_metadata_preconditions_ok`, which compares `lease_expires_at` with `authoritative_now_ms` (the engine commit clock, never a request field); `validate_cas_work_item_metadata_request` (lease.rs:198) stays the shape check; tests live in crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/lease/tests.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/lease/tests.rs::fenced_cas_before_exact_and_after_expiry` (L2) covers the three instants and absent expiry. `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::fenced_cas_ignores_caller_clock` (L3) sends a future caller clock and asserts refusal. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101` | **Downtime work is an ontology that drives the engine scheduler (rollup).** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. The downtime ontology declares job types, priorities, budgets, windows and preconditions. The scheduler reads the ontology and enqueues eligible downtime jobs in each window. Foreground work preempts downtime jobs. A served read, `DowntimeSelections`, returns the recorded selections. The field `intent` names a registered `JobIntent` of `medulla-jobs`. A job type has at most 8 preconditions of at most 4 KiB each. `WindowKind` is `idle` or `declared`. The shapes file refuses a `dw:JobType` without `dw:Budget`. Caller chain: startup registration in `server_startup` (crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs) → `medulla_jobs::downtime::DowntimeJobType` validation → shapes check of the `core:downtime-work@1` source loaded by `current_core_catalog` (crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/schema_sources.rs). Shape: new files crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs (types `DowntimeJobType`, `DowntimeWindow`, `WindowKind`) and crates/medulla-core/ontology/downtime_work-v1.ttl plus `downtime_work-v1.shapes.ttl`; reuse `ResourceBudget` (crates/medulla-jobs/src/model.rs:57) and the intent registry in crates/medulla-jobs/src/intent.rs; the module is declared in crates/medulla-jobs/src/lib.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs::tests::job_type_round_trips` (L1) round-trips every field and refuses a job type without a budget. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Code: `medulla_jobs::downtime::select_for_window(types, window, last_run, eval) -> DowntimeSelection`, pure, with no input or output. The `eval` argument is a trait `PreconditionEval`. An idle window opens when the foreground queue is empty and requests stay under `knob downtime.idle_max_rps` for `knob downtime.idle_quiet_secs`. A declared window opens at its start time. The rule `priority_then_budget_fit` orders eligible types by priority, then by oldest last run, then by IRI. It selects types while the summed budgets fit the window budget. A false precondition skips the type. Knobs: `knob downtime.idle_max_rps` default 1, [0, 100], requests per second. `knob downtime.idle_quiet_secs` default 60, [10, 3600], seconds. `knob downtime.window_cpu_permille` default 250, [10, 1000]. `knob downtime.window_memory_bytes` default 2 GiB, [256 MiB, 64 GiB]. Selection costs O(t log t) for t types. Caller chain: `DowntimeTick` in crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs `run_tick` → `medulla_jobs::downtime::select_for_window`. Shape: pure function and the `PreconditionEval` trait in crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs; it takes the window budget and the foreground-activity reading as plain arguments so it needs no server crate; the sort key is (priority desc, last_run asc, IRI asc) with `ResourceBudget.total` summed against the window budget. | Test: `crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs::tests::priority_then_budget_fit` (L1) runs three types in priority order and skips one with a false precondition. Verified by the root L4 test. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.5` | **Evidence.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Each tick that finds a candidate writes one node `DowntimeSelection{window, candidates, chosen, skipped}` with one reason per skipped type. The reasons are `precondition_false`, `budget_does_not_fit` and `window_closed`. The node commits through the mutation gateway into the commons graph, in one `MutationBatch` with the job submissions. The batch prunes the oldest nodes beyond 1,000. Each job records the budget it used in its `medulla-jobs` result. The node is also written as `dw:Selection` graph facts, so a served `Sparql` read answers `MEDULLA-CQ-ORCH-R011.2`. The served read `DowntimeSelections` returns the newest selections with a cursor. An idle tick with no candidate writes nothing. Caller chain: `run_tick` (crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs) → mutation gateway `MutationBatch` commit through the graph seam `GraphApi` (crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest.rs:27) → served `Method::Sparql` read for MEDULLA-CQ-ORCH-R011.2. Shape: `DowntimeSelection` node type in crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs; the pruning of nodes beyond 1,000 rides in the same batch as the job submissions; reasons are the closed set `precondition_false`, `budget_does_not_fit`, `window_closed`; the read `DowntimeSelections` pages with `crates/medulla-types/src/keyset_page.rs`. | Test: `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs::tests::selection_recorded` (L3) reads the selection and the budget use of each job through `DowntimeSelections`. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102` | **The engine serves its key-value modality as the memory read and save methods (rollup).** `KvGet`, `KvPut`, `KvDelete`, `KvScan` and `KvCas` are stable, wire-callable methods of the generated client contract. Each has request and result schemas and the `kv:read` or `kv:write` scope. Arbora binds `memory.read` and `memory.write` (`ARBORA-OPS-R024.4`) to them. The served graph-memory methods cover the rest: `Consolidate`, `Reinforce`, `DecayMemories`, `SummariesAtLevel` and trajectories. `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085` still owns versioned document sync with retained conflicts. This row adds no second memory model. Children `.1` to `.6`. | Satisfied only when `.1` to `.6` are verified. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.1` | **Served contract.** The five KV methods are `Stable` and carry the Python consumer profile. The generated contract therefore marks them `is_wire_callable: true`, and the Python client binds them. `KvGet` answers `Option<KvEntry{value, version}>`. `KvPut` answers `KvWriteReceipt{version}`. `KvDelete` answers whether the key existed. `KvScan` answers `KvScanPage{entries, next_cursor}`. `KvCas` answers `KvCasOutcome{swapped, version}`. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `kv_get`, `kv_put`, `kv_delete`, `kv_scan`, `kv_cas` (dispatch_handlers.rs:211-270). Shape: method variants and result types in crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/storage_wire/kv.rs and crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/result_contract/storage.rs; stability and the Python profile come from the policy rows in crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs; no new file. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_dispatch_put_get_scan_delete_cas` round-trips every method through the real dispatch. The generated contract lists all five as wire-callable. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.2` | **Scope and authorization.** A private key's store namespace is an opaque digest of tenant, principal, request graph and namespace. All four come from the verified request; no body field widens them. No Redis, S3 or KV-cache adapter namespace can alias a served one. Reads require `kv:read` or the coarse `kg:read`. Writes require `kv:write` or the coarse `kg:write`. The request boundary refuses a missing scope before any row is read. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `KvCall::namespace` (dispatch_handlers.rs:116) → `wire_namespace` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs:27); scope names are checked at `is_kv_method` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:680). Shape: the namespace digest is `CarrierAuthority::namespace("kv-namespace", ..)` over graph and local namespace; `resolve_kv_store` (dispatch_handlers.rs) refuses before any row read; scopes `kv:read` and `kv:write` are listed in the policy rows of crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_keys_are_scoped_by_graph_and_writes_need_kv_write`: the same key in another graph is absent. A `kv:read`-only caller reads, and its write is refused with the row unchanged. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.3` | **Versioned compare-and-swap and bounded paginated scan.** Every served write stamps the namespace's mutation-kernel commit version. That version never repeats, even after a delete and re-create. `KvCas` swaps only when the stored version equals `expected_version`; `0` means absent. A refused `KvCas` reports the current version. `KvScan` returns at most 1000 rows (default 100) and 4 MiB per page, in key order. Its exclusive-start cursor must lie inside the prefix. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `kv_cas` (dispatch_handlers.rs:270) and `kv_scan` (dispatch_handlers.rs:257) → `versioned_write` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs:117) and `scan_redb_page` (wire.rs:236). Shape: the version sequence uses `next_version(stamp, current)` (wire.rs:73) so it never repeats; page bounds are the constants `DEFAULT_WIRE_SCAN_LIMIT`, `MAX_WIRE_SCAN_LIMIT` and `MAX_WIRE_SCAN_PAGE_BYTES` (wire.rs:20-22); `PageBuilder` (wire.rs:272) stops at the byte cap; `scan_start` (wire.rs:218) refuses a cursor outside the prefix. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_dispatch_put_get_scan_delete_cas` covers stale-version refusal, create-if-absent and no A-B-A after delete. `::kv_scan_pages_are_bounded_and_resume_from_the_cursor` reads 25 keys in three pages and refuses a foreign cursor. `crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs::tests::versions_never_repeat_across_delete_and_recreate` covers the row codec. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.4` | **Durable, audited, replayable mutations.** `KvPut`, `KvDelete` and `KvCas` each commit as one native MutationBatch in `kv.redb`. Their durability domain is `KvRedb`, and they commit before they acknowledge. One transaction holds the row, ledger, fence, idempotency record and outbox. They are `audited`: a digest-only `KV_PUT`, `KV_DELETE` or `KV_CAS` line plus the durable batch ledger. They emit no graph CDC, because native outbox rows are the projection authority. `KvDelete` is idempotent; `KvPut` and `KvCas` are not. All three replay by `OperationIdentity`. A fresh nonce with the same operation key returns the stored verdict without a second write. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `KvCall::mutate` (dispatch_handlers.rs:131) → `compile_kv_batch` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:613) → `write_kv_row` (kv.rs:186) → `medulla_kernel::audit::audit_line`. Shape: one `MutationBatch` per call, committed to kv.redb through `swap_in_owner_write` (kv.rs:157); the `audited` flag and `DurabilityDomain::KvRedb` are set in crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs; replay identity comes from `OperationIdentity` in the batch compile of crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_fresh_nonce_replays_stable_operation_without_duplicate_write`; `crates/medulla-capabilities/tests/consistency` `audited_matches_audit_rs_exactly`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.5` | **Hot-path proof.** The entry point is the served, authenticated dispatch against a durable `kv.redb`. The test sends 400 sequential `KvPut`/`KvGet` pairs of 256-byte values. `KvGet` must finish within 5 ms at p50 and 20 ms at p99. `KvPut` must finish within 25 ms at p50 and 500 ms at p99. Both include envelope verification. `KvPut` also includes the durable commit. On R820 in a debug build, `KvGet` measured 1012 µs at p50 and 1731 µs at p99. There `KvPut` measured 3931 µs at p50 and 7006 µs at p99. On a hosted CI runner, `KvGet` measured 530 µs at p50 and 905 µs at p99. There `KvPut` measured 2151 µs at p50 and 110589 µs at p99. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `kv_put` (dispatch_handlers.rs:218) and `kv_get` (dispatch_handlers.rs:211) after envelope verification in `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158). Shape: no production change: the budget test `kv_served_hot_path_meets_latency_budgets` in crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs measures percentiles with the same dispatch helper as the other tests there; the fsync-dominated `KvPut` figure comes from the single commit of `write_kv_row`. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_served_hot_path_meets_latency_budgets` prints and asserts the measured p50 and p99. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.6` | **Opt-in shared namespace.** Every KV method takes `shared`, which defaults to `false`. A private key stays scoped to the verified tenant, principal, request graph and namespace. `shared: true` drops only the principal. Every principal of the verified tenant and request graph then sees one row. The tenant owns its kernel scope, so writers from different principals advance one version sequence. A shared `KvPut`, `KvDelete` or `KvCas` needs the separate `kv:shared:write` action or `kg:admin`. The coarse `kg:write` and `kv:write` grants are refused. A shared read needs `kv:read`. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `names_shared` (dispatch_handlers.rs:166) sets `KvCall.shared` → `KvCall::scope_owner` (dispatch_handlers.rs:122) and `wire_namespace` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs:27). Shape: `shared` is a field of every `Method::Kv*` variant in crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_06.rs and method_07.rs; a shared namespace is `opaque_coordinator_key("kv-shared-namespace", tenant_scope, ..)` and its kernel scope owner is `CarrierAuthority::tenant_scope`; `kv:shared:write` is accepted at `is_kv_method` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:683). | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_shared_namespace_spans_principals_and_needs_kv_shared_write`: one principal's private key is absent for another. A second principal reads a shared write, which is not the writer's private row. A coarse-write principal is refused. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R750.750` | **Model.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. An agent turn is a WorkItem with `kind` `agent_turn`. Its `input_ref` is the `request_ref` of an `AdmittedRequest` (`MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R820.1`). The session lock is the conversation head of `ConversationTurnAdmission`: a second turn on a context needs the exact preceding run. The carrier is the claim capability minted by `MintWorkItemClaimCapability` and checked by `VerifyWorkItemClaimCapability` in `src/redb_store/work_item_capability`. Add the row key `cancel_requested_at_ms: Option<u64>` to `NATIVE_WORK_ITEM_ROW_KEYS` in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_read.rs`. Add `cancel_requested: bool` to the `RenewWorkItemLease` result in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/result_contract/coordination/work_items.rs`. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_read/tests.rs::cancel_requested_round_trips` (L1) round-trips the row key and the renew result.
