| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R750.751` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. Enqueue counts the tenant in-flight rows with `count_submit_work_item_tenant_inflight` in `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/submit.rs`. It refuses when the count reaches `max_tenant_in_flight`. Claim and commit stay idempotent by `idempotency_key`. `CancelWorkItem` on a leased item with an unexpired lease returns `InFlight` and stamps `cancel_requested_at_ms` in `cancel.rs`. The next `RenewWorkItemLease` renews the lease and returns `cancel_requested` true. The worker stops at its next step boundary and calls `CommitWorkItemResult` with status `cancelled`. When the lease expires first, `claim_reclaim_expired_lease` in `claim.rs` retires the item as `cancelled` and never returns it to `ready`. The cancel grace is the remaining lease time, so no new knob exists. Caller chain: served `Method::SubmitWorkItem`, `CancelWorkItem`, `RenewWorkItemLease` and `CommitWorkItemResult` → wire `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158) → `apply_native_work_item_family_operation` (crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:461) → `apply_work_item_rows` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs:38) → `count_submit_work_item_tenant_inflight` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/submit.rs:223), `apply_cancel_work_item_row` (cancel.rs:116), `apply_renew_work_item_lease_row` (lease.rs:13), `claim_reclaim_expired_lease` (claim.rs:111). Shape: extend the existing `cancel_requested_at_ms` property writes in cancel.rs and the renew result in lease.rs (`lease_renewed`, lease.rs:97) with the `cancel_requested` flag; the retire-as-cancelled branch is a new arm next to `retire_exhausted_lease` (claim.rs:48); the result type gains the field in crates/medulla-work-types/src/native_control.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/cancel.rs::tests::in_flight_cancel_stamps_request_and_renew_reports_it` (L1). Verified by the root L4 test. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R750.752` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. Enqueue beyond `max_tenant_in_flight` refuses with the typed code `WORK_QUEUE_FULL` and writes nothing. A second turn on a context with a stale head refuses with the conflict of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.3`. A claim capability that cannot be verified refuses as `ACCESS_DENIED` for every verify failure (`unauthorized`, `malformed` and any other denial of the capability verifier); the served answer never names the reason, and the privacy-safe reason (the verify denial class) is written to the audit record only. A cancel of a terminal item returns `Noop` and changes nothing. `WORK_QUEUE_FULL` is registered in `ServerErrorCode`. Caller chain: served `Method::SubmitWorkItem` and `CancelWorkItem` → wire `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158) → `apply_native_work_item_family_operation` (crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:461) → `apply_work_item_rows` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs:38) → `resolve_submit_work_item_max_inflight` (submit.rs:67) and `reserve` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/conversation.rs:123) → refusal mapped by the method-error layer of crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch. Shape: `WORK_QUEUE_FULL` is a new variant in `ServerErrorCode` (crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs:8) raised where the in-flight count meets `max_tenant_in_flight` in submit.rs; every capability verify failure maps to the existing `ACCESS_DENIED` at the method-error layer, and the verify denial class goes to the audit record only; cancel of a terminal item is `cancel_status_refusal` (cancel.rs:33). | Test: `tests/durable/test_agent_turn_queue_served.py::test_full_queue_stale_head_and_bad_capability_refuse` (L4) asserts each code and unchanged state, with a cross-tenant case, and that both a forged (`unauthorized`) and a truncated (`malformed`) capability answer `ACCESS_DENIED` with the reason present in the audit record and absent from the answer. |

Add `EffectReceipt{job_id, attempt, request_ref, principal_ref, outcome, receipt_ref, committed_at}` in the same file, where `attempt` is the `u64` attempt of the `Started` claim and `receipt_ref` is a `ReceiptId`, a newtype over `sha256:` plus 64 lowercase hex characters of the SHA-256 of the canonical receipt. Receipts live in their own native table, not in the work-item row: `work_item_effect_receipts` (`EFFECT_RECEIPTS: TableDefinition<(&str, u64), &[u8]>` in `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/effect.rs`), keyed by `(work_item_id, attempt)`, value the MessagePack encoding of `EffectReceipt`. The table joins the graph-shard owner table list in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/graph_shard.rs` with a new `GRAPH_SHARD_BEFORE_EFFECT_RECEIPTS` layout predecessor in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs`, the same pattern as `work_item_run_checkpoints`. `WorkItemView` gains `effect_receipt: Option<ReceiptId>`, read from that table by `(work_item_id, current attempt)`, and `None` until a receipt exists. The fixed native row key set is unchanged by receipts: only `effect_phase` joins it. `outcome` is the closed set `applied`, `not_applied` and `compensated`. Add `AdmittedRequest{request_ref, tenant, session_ref, payload_digest, bytes}` as an immutable record stored once under its `request_ref`. `request_ref` is `sha256:` plus 64 lowercase hex characters of the SHA-256 of the canonical request. `bytes` is at most 1 MiB, a `Fixed` limit. One receipt exists for each `(work_item_id, attempt)`, which the table key enforces, and a job ID belongs to one key. Add `effect_phase` to `WorkItemView`. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R820.4` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. A receipt whose job ID, request reference or principal differs from the work item refuses as `EFFECT_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`. A second, different receipt under one `(work_item_id, attempt)` key of `work_item_effect_receipts` refuses as `EFFECT_RECEIPT_CONFLICT`. A receipt whose `attempt` differs from the item's `Started` attempt refuses as `EFFECT_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`. A start mark without a live claim refuses as `WORK_ITEM_CLAIM_STALE`. A request read from another tenant or session returns not found. An admitted request whose bytes do not match its reference refuses as `ADMITTED_REQUEST_DIGEST_MISMATCH`. A request above 1 MiB refuses as `ADMITTED_REQUEST_TOO_LARGE`. All codes are registered in `ServerErrorCode`. A refusal changes no durable state. Caller chain: served `RecordEffectReceipt`, `MarkWorkItemEffectStarted`, `SubmitAdmittedRequest`, `GetAdmittedRequest` → wire `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158) → `apply_native_work_item_family_operation` (crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:461) → `apply_work_item_rows` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs:38) → the arms of effect.rs. Shape: six new variants in crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs; refusals return before any table write, mirroring `unchanged_transition` (lease.rs:138); a foreign tenant or session read returns `None` like `read_work_item` for another tenant. | Test: `tests/durable/test_uncertain_effect_served.py::test_refusals_leave_state_unchanged` (L4) asserts each code, an unchanged item, and a foreign-tenant read that returns not found. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700` | **Retention cleanup removes aged documents and orphans (rollup).** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.1` | **Model.** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. `RetentionPolicy{age_ms, batch, holds}` exists in `crates/medulla-types/src/retention_policy.rs` and refuses zero age, a batch outside [1, 10000] and an empty hold list. Add `RetentionCleanupRunRequest{graph, dry_run, cursor: Option<String>}`, `RetentionCleanupReport{scanned, removed, held_live_reference, held_legal_hold, next_cursor}` and `RetentionCleanupRecord{graph, cursor_start, cursor_end, scanned, removed, held_live_reference, held_legal_hold, policy_digest, at_ms}` in new `crates/medulla-types/src/retention_cleanup.rs`. The default batch is 1,000. The default age is unset: no policy means no sweep. Caller chain: `RetentionPolicy` is parsed by the tick in `server_startup::retention_cleanup_tick` (crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs, new beside `sealed_expiry_tick` at line 930) and by the `RetentionCleanupRun` handler. Shape: validation lives in `RetentionPolicy` and `RetentionPolicyError` (crates/medulla-types/src/retention_policy.rs:24,31); the new request, report and record types go in a new crates/medulla-types/src/retention_cleanup.rs declared in lib.rs, encoded with the wire codec of crates/medulla-method-codec. | Test: `crates/medulla-types/src/retention_cleanup.rs::tests::report_and_record_round_trip` (L1) round-trips both records through the wire codec. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. Code: `cleanup_graph(core, policy, cursor, now_ms) -> RetentionCleanupReport` in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs`. Document candidates come from `GraphCore::get_nodes_by_label_page("Document", ..)` in id order after the cursor, and match when `updated_at` is older than `age_ms`. Orphan candidates come from `GraphCore::node_ids()` sorted after the cursor. A node matches when `in_degree` and `out_degree` are 0. It also needs no `owner_ref` metadata key and a `created_at` older than `age_ms`. A step examines at most `batch` candidates and returns `next_cursor` as the last examined id. Each step removes through one `Method::BatchUpdate` of `RemoveNode` operations, so it is one `MutationBatch`. Removal releases the blob holders of the node. Blob garbage collection then applies the grace period of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R050`. The cost is O(n) ids per step plus O(batch) examined nodes. Caller chain: `RetentionCleanupRun` handler arm and the periodic tick (through the `GraphApi` seam, crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest.rs:27) → `cleanup_graph` in crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs → `GraphCore::get_nodes_by_label_page` (used at crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/nodes.rs:192) and `GraphCore::node_ids` (crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_readthrough.rs:63) → one `Method::BatchUpdate`. Shape: the file sits in the graph handler crate so both entry points reach it without one handler crate calling another; the removal batch is built by the same helper that `RemoveNode` writes use in graph_ops/nodes.rs; blob holders are released by the existing node-removal path. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs::tests::step_examines_batch_in_id_order` (L1) runs fixtures of 10, 1,000 and 100,000 nodes and asserts the batch bound and cursor order. Verified by the root L4 test. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.4` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. The engine never removes a node with a live inbound edge from outside the batch. It never removes a node whose metadata sets `legal_hold` to true. The hold checks are `Fixed` and cannot be disabled. A policy without a hold check refuses at registration as `RETENTION_HOLD_CHECK_REQUIRED`. A policy with zero age or an out-of-range batch refuses as `RETENTION_POLICY_INVALID`. A request without the scope `retention:run` refuses as `ACCESS_DENIED`. A refusal leaves durable state unchanged. Both codes are registered in `ServerErrorCode`. Caller chain: `RetentionCleanupRun` handler arm → `cleanup_graph` (crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs) hold checks → refusal through `ServerErrorCode`. Shape: `in_degree` and `out_degree` checks and the `legal_hold` metadata test run per candidate before the batch is built; policy registration refuses in `RetentionPolicy::validate` (crates/medulla-types/src/retention_policy.rs); two new codes in crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs; the `retention:run` scope is checked at the request boundary. | Test: `tests/durable/test_retention_cleanup_served.py::test_refusals_leave_state_unchanged` (L4) asserts each code and an unchanged graph. It adds a cross-tenant case. |

The counter `medulla_retention_cleanup_removed_total{reason}` uses reasons `aged_document` and `orphan`. A dry run writes nothing and returns the report. A read of the record reproduces the report that the step returned. Caller chain: `cleanup_graph` (crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs) builds the record node in the same `Method::BatchUpdate` as the removals. Shape: `RetentionCleanupRecord` from crates/medulla-types/src/retention_cleanup.rs; pruning keeps the newest 100 per graph in the same batch; counter `medulla_retention_cleanup_removed_total` registered in crates/medulla-server/src/metrics and incremented by the tick and the handler caller after commit. | Test: `tests/durable/test_retention_cleanup_served.py::test_record_reproduces_report` (L4) reads the stored record after a step and compares it to the returned report. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800` | **Cache tier admission, rebuild-cost eviction and answer-cache freshness (rollup).** Subsystem: durable kernel, cache tier. Entry points: the answer cache of `crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs` and the shared block store of `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs`. Caller: `cache_dep_result` called from `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs` → `PolicyTier::admit` and `PolicyTier::lookup` in `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs`. `PolicyTier` decides which prompt-prefix and answer entries are worth caching in each tier. It uses reuse rate and rebuild cost and evicts by value density. It expires answers by the volatility class of their dependencies. Children `.1` to `.6` cover model, behavior, wiring, refusal, evidence and hot-path proof. Consumers: `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R017`, `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R018` and `MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R018.2`. | The root L4 test is `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_answer_entry_invalidated_when_dependency_advances`. It binds every child. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.1` | **Model.** `CacheEntryMeta{key_digest, tier, bytes, hits, rebuild_cost_ms, deps:[GenerationRef]}` and `TierPolicy{max_bytes, min_worth}` in `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs`. Caller chain: `PolicyTier::admit` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs:137) is called by the answer cache `cache_dep_result` (crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs:110) and by `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:206). Shape: `CacheEntryMeta`, `GenerationRef` and `TierPolicy` already exist in crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs (lines 19, 26, 47) with `CacheEntryMeta::worth` (line 37); tests extend crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs. | Planned: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::low_worth_entry_is_not_admitted`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.2` | **Behavior.** `worth = hits * rebuild_cost_ms / bytes`. Admission needs worth at or above `min_worth`. Eviction removes the lowest worth first from a heap in O(log N). An answer entry is invalid once any dependency generation advances. It is also invalid once its volatility window passes. Caller chain: `PolicyTier::admit` (policy.rs:137), `PolicyTier::lookup` (policy.rs:179) and `PolicyTier::evict_lowest` (policy.rs:206), reached from `cache_dep_result` (crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs:110). Shape: `CacheEntryMeta::worth` (policy.rs:37) is the one formula; the eviction heap is a `BinaryHeap` keyed on worth inside `PolicyTier`; `set_generation` (policy.rs:114) advances a dependency; tests are `lowest_worth_is_evicted_first` and `dependency_advance_and_volatility_window_end_the_hit` in crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs. | Planned: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::lowest_worth_is_evicted_first`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.3` | **Wiring.** Subsystem: durable kernel, cache tier. `PolicyTier` has no production caller today. Caller 1: `cache_dep_result` in `crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs` admits each answer through `PolicyTier::admit`. The call passes the tenant and the dependency generations of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R017` and `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R018`. It sets `rebuild_cost_ms` to the measured execution time. `ResultCache::get` reads through `PolicyTier::lookup`. Caller 2: `SharedKvStoreBackend::put` in `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs` admits each prompt-prefix block. A write that advances a dependency calls `PolicyTier::set_generation`. Knobs: `knob kvcache.min_worth` default 0.001, [0, 1000], milliseconds saved per byte per hit. `knob kvcache.answer_max_bytes` default 256 MiB, [1 MiB, 64 GiB], bytes. The cache is a pure optimization: a refusal is a miss. Caller chain: served query dispatch → `cache_dep_result` (crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs:110) → `PolicyTier::admit` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs:137); HTTP `PUT /kv/<hash>` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs:201) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (shared_store.rs:206) → `PolicyTier::admit`. Shape: add `medulla-kvcache` as a dependency of crates/medulla-query-cache and hold a `PolicyTier` inside `ResultCache` (result_cache.rs); both knobs are read where the tier is built; `rebuild_cost_ms` is the elapsed time the dispatch already measures around execution. | Test: `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_answer_entry_invalidated_when_dependency_advances` (L4) serves one cached query, advances a dependency, and asserts the next call recomputes. `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::dependency_advance_invalidates_answer` (L1) covers the policy. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.4` | **Refusal.** Three inputs return a miss with no content. The first is an entry larger than the tier. The second is a dependency list that names an unknown generation. The third is a read for another tenant's key. The engine never returns a stale hit. Caller chain: `PolicyTier::admit` (policy.rs:137) and `PolicyTier::lookup` (policy.rs:179) with a tenant argument, reached from `cache_dep_result` and `ResultCache::get`. Shape: refusals return `None` or `false`, never an error; the unknown-generation check reads the map `set_generation` fills (policy.rs:114); tenant isolation keys every entry by `(tenant, key_digest)` as `contains` (policy.rs:130) already does. | Planned: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::oversize_entry_is_refused`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.5` | **Evidence.** Subsystem: durable kernel, cache tier. Add `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/metrics.rs` with the counter `medulla_kvcache_policy_total{tier,event}`. The events are `admitted`, `refused`, `evicted`, `invalidated`, `hit` and `miss`. The route `GET /kv/stats` in `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs` also returns the hit ratio and a replayable trace of the newest 1,024 decisions. Each trace entry holds the key digest, the event, the worth and the refusal reason. It never holds a key or a value. The trace holds no tenant data beyond a tenant digest. Caller chain: `GET /kv/stats` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs, stats probe near line 362) → counters updated by `PolicyTier` callers in `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (shared_store.rs:206). Shape: the counter lives in the wire-msg crate that owns the HTTP surface, not in a server module; the trace is a fixed-size ring of 1,024 entries kept O(1) so the stats probe stays O(1); entries hold `key_digest`, event, worth and reason only. | Test: `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_stats_report_policy_counters_and_trace` (L4) drives admissions and evictions and compares the counters and trace. `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::foreign_tenant_key_misses` (L1) covers tenant isolation. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.6` | **Hot-path proof.** Subsystem: durable kernel, cache tier. Entry point: the served query path of `.3` and the nightly benchmark `crates/medulla-kvcache/benches/tier_policy.rs`. The benchmark runs 1 million admit and lookup operations. Budget: lookup p50 under 5 microseconds, lookup p99 under 50 microseconds, admission overhead under 10 percent of an unpoliced insert. The benchmark never gates a pull request. The served test proves the behavior, not the speed. Caller chain: served query path of R800.3 → `PolicyTier::lookup` and `PolicyTier::admit` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs:179,137). Shape: new bench crates/medulla-kvcache/benches/tier_policy.rs (add a `[[bench]]` entry in crates/medulla-kvcache/Cargo.toml) running one million operations; the served test proves behaviour only. | Test: `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_policy_admits_hot_answer_and_refuses_cold_one` (L4). The nightly receipt (L6) meets the budget. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4` | **Run ownership binds at native admission.** Subsystem: work items. Caller: served `Method::SubmitWorkItem` with a run admission request → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs::apply_work_item_rows` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/run_owner.rs::admit_owner`. A single-run admission records the verified caller as the owner inside the WorkItem transaction. The WorkItem ID is `work_item_id(graph, tenant, run_id)`. A caller-provided ID, a batch run admission and a generic write that changes an owner binding all refuse with `ACCESS_DENIED`. A duplicate admission refuses with `CONFLICT`. Replay authority stays mandatory. Children `.2` to `.7` keep recovery, replay and performance. Consumer: ARBORA-A2A-R001.2. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-store-tests/src/split_tests/store_run_owner.rs::admission_binds_owner_and_refuses_duplicate_forged_and_generic_writes` (L2). `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::run_admission_through_signed_dispatch` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.1` | **Recover immutable run submission records.** `GetRunWorkItem` reads the WorkItem and original submission from one durable snapshot. Graph, tenant, run and verified owner select the record. Missing and foreign records return null. Missing or malformed historical evidence returns non-retryable `RUN_ADMISSION_UNAVAILABLE`. Completion, lease expiry and restart preserve the original submission. Generic writes cannot plant, replace or remove it. Reading grants no execution authority. Caller chain: served `Method::GetRunWorkItem` → `route_owner_bound_work_item_reads` (crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/native_routes.rs:305) → `read_admission` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/run_owner.rs:70). Shape: one snapshot read in run_owner.rs using `snapshot_row` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/read.rs:36); the immutable binding is guarded by `row_guard.rs` so generic writers cannot plant, replace or remove it; types in crates/medulla-work-types/src/run_history.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-store-tests/src/split_tests/store_run_owner.rs::run_admission_recovery_preserves_original_request` (L2) and `::run_admission_recovery_refuses_missing_evidence` (L2) cover restart, owner isolation and invalid evidence; `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::run_admission_owner_read_survives_native_changes_and_restart` (L3) covers authenticated dispatch. Point lookup costs O(log N + B) time and O(B) memory for B bounded submission bytes. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.2` | **Owner-bound recovery meets a 10 ms p99.** Subsystem: work items. Caller: `benches/redb_write_latency_bench.rs` group `run_owner_recovery` → `run_owner.rs::read_admission`. The bench recovers an owner-bound original submission from a store of 10 000 WorkItems. Budget: p99 under 10 ms on the soak host. The bench runs in the nightly job of `ci.yml` and never gates a pull request. Each result records workload, hardware, build profile and p50, p99. Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.4. Caller chain: nightly job → `benches/redb_write_latency_bench.rs` group `run_owner_recovery` → `read_admission` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/run_owner.rs:70). Shape: add the group to the existing benches/redb_write_latency_bench.rs, seeding 10 000 WorkItems through the same shard fixture its other groups use and timing `read_admission`; result recorded with `BenchmarkClaim` fields. | Test: `benches/redb_write_latency_bench.rs::run_owner_recovery` (nightly bench) fails the nightly job when p99 reaches 10 ms. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.5` | **Current plan publication and readback.** Subsystem: work items. Caller: served `Method::PublishCurrentPlan` and `Method::GetCurrentPlan` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs::apply_work_item_rows` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/current_plan.rs::publish` and `read_current`. Publication needs the verified owner and the live exact fence. Revision zero is an empty first revision. Later revisions strictly increase. Replay keeps the expiry. Reads return one snapshot of owner and lease. Receipts reuse the WorkItem `MutationBatch` identity. Budget: p99 under 10 ms for 1024 items and 1 MiB. Refusals: `ACCESS_DENIED` owner, `CONFLICT` fence, expiry or revision. Children `.1` to `.4`. Consumers: ARBORA-RELEASE-R003.4.2 and ARBORA-RELEASE-R003.4.9. | Test: `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::current_plan_native_publish_read_and_receipt_replay` (L3) exists. `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/current_plan/tests.rs::current_plan_publication_readback` (L2) covers ordering. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R030.1` | **Issuer-bound host-start grant.** Subsystem: control leases. Caller: served `Method::IssueHostStartGrant` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs::apply_work_item_rows` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/control_lease/host_start.rs`. The caller needs service scope `host:issue-start`. Generic `IssueControlLease` refuses kind `host.start`. Engine time bounds expiry to the asserted session expiry and to 24 hours. The row stores the beneficiary, session, workspace, configuration, interface and policy assertions. Issuer provenance comes from the authenticated batch actor and sits outside the grant JSON. Limits: grant 64 KiB, reference 512 bytes, one inserted row, fixed. Generic consumption refuses. CAS revoke and expire only remove availability. Refusals: `ACCESS_DENIED` scope, `CONTROL_LEASE_KIND_RESERVED`. Consumer: ARBORA-HOST-R010.7.1, which owns session policy and startup. Bench: `benches/redb_write_latency_bench.rs` group `host_start_issue`, p99 under 5 ms, nightly. | Tests: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/control_lease/host_start/tests.rs` (L2) covers bounds, stamping, collisions and consumption refusal. `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item/host_start_tests.rs::lease_only_caller_is_refused_the_issue_scope` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.5` | **Tier ladder from accelerator memory to durable storage.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `PUT /kv/<hash>` → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` → `TieredCache::put` in `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs`. `SharedKvStoreBackend` holds a `TieredCache<String, Block>` whose cold tier is `RedbColdStore` (`crates/medulla-kvcache/src/cold.rs`, feature `durable`). Server config keys set the budgets. `MEDULLA_KVCACHE_HOT_BYTES` defaults to 1 GiB. `MEDULLA_KVCACHE_WARM_BYTES` defaults to 4 GiB. `MEDULLA_KVCACHE_COLD_BYTES` defaults to 64 GiB. Each range is [64 MiB, 1 TiB]. Byte pressure demotes the lowest-scored block one rung: hot, compressed warm, durable cold. A hit promotes the block to hot. A demotion moves the bytes and never drops them. The connector offloads from accelerator memory through `medulla/kvcache/connector.py`. Caller chain: HTTP `PUT /kv/<hash>` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs:201, `KvCacheStore::put`) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:206) → `TieredCache::put` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs:319). Shape: budgets are read where `SharedKvStoreBackend` is built; the cold rung is `RedbColdStore` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/cold.rs, feature `durable`); compression is crates/medulla-kvcache/src/compress.rs; the new test file is crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_ladder.rs and the served test goes under tests/kvcache_http_served.rs. | Tests: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_ladder.rs::demote_then_promote_keeps_bytes` (L2) fills each tier past budget and reads every block back byte-identical. `tests/kvcache_http_served.rs::over_budget_put_demotes_and_get_promotes` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.6` | **Graph-guided paging policy.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `PUT /kv/version/<n>` (the graph write hook) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::page_in_ranked` → `TieredCache::apply_importance_scores` (`tiered.rs`). On a version bump the backend recomputes importance with `graph_importance::pagerank_importance` and applies it. The demotion score is `recency + knob kvcache.importance_weight * importance`, default 1.0, range [0, 10], unit ratio (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179). A hit prefetches cold blocks whose graph neighbors the request reads (`get_with_graph_prefetch`). Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.5, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179.1. Caller chain: `PUT /kv/version/<n>` (kvcache_http/mod.rs, version-bump route) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::page_in_ranked` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:337) → `TieredCache::apply_importance_scores` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs:289); a hit uses `get_with_graph_prefetch` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs:440). Shape: scores come from `graph_importance::pagerank_importance` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/graph_importance.rs:35, opt-in feature `graph`); the demotion score adds `knob kvcache.importance_weight` times importance to the recency term inside `TieredCache`; test file crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/graph_paging.rs. | Tests: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/graph_paging.rs::central_blocks_stay_hot_and_neighbors_prefetch` (L2) exists. `tests/kvcache_http_served.rs::version_bump_refreshes_importance_and_central_block_outlives_peripheral` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.7` | **Long-context coherence across tiers.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `GET /kv/<hash>` → `TieredCache::get`. The tier never serves a block under another model version or token-prefix hash. The test runs in the nightly job and is marked ignored for pull requests. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.8` | **Tier refusals.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `PUT` and `GET /kv/<hash>` → `TieredCache`. A page-in whose digest differs from the stored digest drops the block and returns `KV_BLOCK_DIGEST_MISMATCH` as HTTP 422. Both codes are registered in the KV-cache error enum of `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/contract.rs`. Caller chain: `PUT` and `GET /kv/<hash>` (kvcache_http/mod.rs) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:206) and `fetch` → `TieredCache::put` and `get` → `RedbColdStore` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/cold.rs). Shape: the two codes are variants of the error enum in crates/medulla-kvcache/src/contract.rs; the HTTP layer maps them to 507 and 422 in kvcache_http/mod.rs; a failed cold write leaves the block in the warm map of `TieredCache`. | Tests: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_ladder.rs::cold_write_failure_and_digest_mismatch_refuse` (L2) asserts each code and an unchanged tier state. `tests/kvcache_http_served.rs::refusal_codes_map_to_507_and_422` (L3). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R860.3` | **RESP listener.** Subsystem: durable kernel, wire listeners. Caller: `spawn_redis_listener` in `src/main.rs` starts `redis_wire::serve` of `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/redis_wire` when the build has feature `redis-wire` and `MEDULLA_REDIS_ADDR` is set. A bare enable token binds `127.0.0.1:6379`. `AUTH <principal> <credential>` resolves the principal through the credential store into a verified request context, the same context a signed request carries; a failed `AUTH` answers `-NOAUTH` and binds nothing. Every served command then enters the dispatch with that context and maps to the served KV methods of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102`: `GET` to `KvGet`, `SET` to `KvPut`, `DEL` to `KvDelete`, `EXISTS` and `MGET` to one `KvGet` per key, and `INCR` to a `KvGet` followed by `KvCas` on the read revision (an integer value; a lost race retries up to 8 times, then answers `-ERR INCR_CONFLICT`; a non-integer value answers `-ERR value is not an integer`). Only these string commands are served. Hash, list, set, sorted-set and TTL commands (`HSET`, `LPUSH`, `SADD`, `ZADD`, `EXPIRE`, `TTL` and their families) are refused with `-ERR RESP_COMMAND_UNSUPPORTED` and change nothing. Each verified principal receives an isolated keyspace: the namespace is the principal's private KV scope, so two principals never see each other's keys. Add the integration test `tests/resp_roundtrip.rs`. The in-module test `eg174_listener_roundtrip_over_tcp` in `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/redis_wire/mod.rs` is the unit proof. Caller chain: `spawn_redis_listener` (src/main.rs:1276) → `medulla_wire_msg::redis_wire::serve` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/redis_wire/mod.rs:2539) → `dispatch` (redis_wire/mod.rs:2048) → `execute_data` (mod.rs:1943). Shape: today `execute_data` calls the KV store of crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs directly through the per-principal namespace of `pseudonymous_broker_actor` (mod.rs:1567). This row changes `AUTH` to build the verified request context from the credential store and the string commands to enter the served KV methods of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102` through the dispatch, so the listener shares the admission and policy path of every other wire. The unsupported families are removed from `execute_data` and answer `RESP_COMMAND_UNSUPPORTED`. The namespace is the principal's private KV scope. The row adds tests/resp_roundtrip.rs. | Test: `tests/resp_roundtrip.rs::set_get_maps_to_kv_methods` (L3) writes with `SET` and reads the value back through `KvGet` under the same principal, covers `DEL`, `EXISTS`, `MGET` and `INCR`, asserts `RESP_COMMAND_UNSUPPORTED` for `HSET`, `LPUSH`, `SADD`, `ZADD` and `EXPIRE` with no state change, and that a second principal does not see the first principal's keys. |

The build needs feature `s3-api` and the variable `MEDULLA_S3_ADDR` must be set. A bare enable token binds `127.0.0.1:9000`. Startup fails closed unless the access and secret credentials are injected at runtime. Put, get, list and delete map to content-addressed blobs and the durable KV listing index. Every request needs `SigV4` authentication. Add the integration test `tests/s3_roundtrip.rs`. Caller chain: `spawn_s3_listener` (src/main.rs:1350) → `medulla_wire_msg::s3::serve` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/s3/mod.rs:1137) → `serve_with_blob_store` (s3/mod.rs:1122) → routes in s3/route.rs. Shape: SigV4 verification is in crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/s3/mod.rs with `calendar.rs`; unsigned requests end at the `AccessDenied` response (mod.rs:909); add tests/s3_roundtrip.rs with a signed client. The client is the dev-dependency `aws-sigv4` (Apache-2.0, minimum version `1`) in `[dev-dependencies]` of the root `Cargo.toml`: it signs each request with `Authorization` header signing, and the test sends the signed request over the `reqwest` already in the tree. The server verifies only the `Authorization` header; presigned query authentication is out of scope and a presigned request is refused with `AccessDenied`. The full AWS SDK is not added. | Test: `tests/s3_roundtrip.rs::put_get_list_delete` (L3) drives each operation through a standard S3-compatible client with signed requests. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R860.5` | **Listener refusals.** Subsystem: durable kernel, wire listeners. Each listener refuses an unauthenticated session with the error form of its protocol. The forms are a PostgreSQL error response, a Bolt `FAILURE`, a RESP `-NOAUTH` error and an S3 `AccessDenied` response. Each listener also checks its startup policy: `validate_startup_policy` refuses a non-loopback address without a configured credential before it binds. A refusal changes no stored state. Depends on: `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R860.1` to `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R860.4`. Caller chain: each listener entry (`serve` of pgwire, bolt_wire, redis_wire, s3) → its authentication step; startup → `validate_startup_policy` (crates/medulla-wire/src/bolt_wire/mod.rs:71) before bind via `spawn_authenticated_wire_listener` (src/main.rs:1156). Shape: refusal forms already exist: RESP `NOAUTH` (redis_wire/mod.rs:1628), S3 `AccessDenied` (s3/mod.rs:909), Bolt `FAILURE` in bolt_wire/auth.rs, PostgreSQL error in pgwire/auth.rs; the new test file tests/wire_listeners_one_store.rs drives all four. | Test: `tests/wire_listeners_one_store.rs::unauthenticated_sessions_refuse` (L3) asserts the refusal on each protocol, the startup refusal on each non-loopback address, and an unchanged store. |

The test reads each record back through the native served method of its modality. It reads the SQL row and the Bolt node with `GetNodes`. It reads the RESP key with `KvGet`. It reads the S3 object with `BlobFetchBegin` and `BlobChunkGet`. It then restarts the binary and reads all four again. The four modalities hold separate records. A record written through one protocol does not appear in another modality. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R862.2` | **Wiring: an explicit command re-binds a copied data directory.** Stage: durable kernel, operator commands. Caller: `medulla-server store-upgrade rebind <data-dir> --confirm` → `OperatorCommand::StoreUpgrade` with new verb `StoreUpgradeVerb::Rebind` in `src/operator_command.rs` → `store_upgrade::rebind` in new `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/rebind.rs`. Code: add `rebind_store_root(path)` to `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/root.rs`. It overwrites the `STORE_ROOT` row with the incarnation derived from the current file, in one write transaction per store. Rule: `rebind` holds the engine lock of `crates/medulla-kernel/src/persist_lock.rs`. For each store from `discover(data_dir)` it runs `validate_recovery_store_read_only` on the source store, read only, and records `recovery_store_fingerprint` of that source. The read-only check applies to the source, not to the copy: a copied store fails it by design, so the check is never run on the copy before the rebind. `recovery_store_fingerprint` covers the data tables only (the ledger tables of `visit_ledger_tables!`) and excludes the scope-binding rows (`STORE_ROOT` and the per-scope bindings), because the rebind rewrites those. The fingerprint is computed on the source and on the copy before the rebind; they must be equal, else `REBIND_FINGERPRINT_CHANGED` names the store and nothing is written. The rebind then runs, and the copy's fingerprint is computed again and must equal the source's; a difference is the same error and names the store. Without `--confirm` it writes nothing and exits 1. A second run reports `nothing to do` and exits 0. The ordinary open still refuses a copied store. Exit codes follow the existing `store-upgrade` table. Refusals: `REBIND_NOT_CONFIRMED`, `REBIND_FINGERPRINT_CHANGED`. Writes: the `STORE_ROOT` row of each store. Depends on: R862.1. Caller chain: `medulla-server store-upgrade rebind` → `OperatorCommand::StoreUpgrade` (src/operator_command.rs:15, dispatch at line 66) → `rebind` in crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/rebind.rs beside `apply` (crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade.rs:75). Shape: `StoreUpgradeVerb::Rebind` is a new variant next to `Inspect` and `Apply` (src/operator_command.rs:22); `rebind_store_root` goes in crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/root.rs; fingerprints use `recovery_store_fingerprint` (crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/backup.rs:20), restricted to data tables (the scope-binding tables are not in the hash) and `validate_recovery_store_read_only` (crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/validate.rs:57); store discovery is `discover.rs` in the same directory. | Test: `tests/operator_command_store_upgrade_cli.rs::rebind_copied_store_then_open` (L3) copies a store and shows the ordinary open refuses it. It runs `rebind --confirm` and reopens with rows kept, with the fingerprint of source and copy equal before and after the rebind. It runs `rebind` again and expects `nothing to do`. Test: `tests/operator_command_store_upgrade_cli.rs::rebind_without_confirm_writes_nothing` (L3). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.3` | **Reserve ordered conversation turns with native run admission.** Optional version-one `ConversationTurnAdmission` binds a context ID, expected head revision and exact preceding run. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.4` | **Repeated run admission replays the retained owner receipt.** Subsystem: work items. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R042.100.1` | **Measurement type and arithmetic.** Subsystem: benchmarks. Caller: `bench-claim admit` → `BenchmarkMeasurement::validate`. The measurement type carries no version suffix and has a `schema_version` field. Identity fields: benchmark case, configuration digest, dataset and query digests, release commit and hardware digest. Revision fields: model, provider, tokenizer and price-table digest. The price table is a versioned declarative table `{resource_kind -> unit, unit_price}` over `cpu_ms`, `bytes_read`, `bytes_written`, `network_bytes`, `model_tokens` and `storage_byte_hours`. It loads from configuration with a SHA-256 content digest. Cost is the sum over resource kinds of usage times `unit_price`. The digest is recorded on every receipt. Timing fields: request start, request end, model-ready time and inference elapsed ms. Count field: full input tokens. Throughput is `full_input_tokens * 1000 / inference_elapsed_ms`. The total uses the price-table currency and minor-unit exponent with round-half-even. Digests are SHA-256 as 64 lowercase hex characters. Caller chain: `bench-claim admit` (new `[[bin]]` in Cargo.toml, src/bin/bench_claim.rs) → `BenchmarkMeasurement::validate`.
